「你是指这个——这个——」麦考尔太太打了个寒噤。「你说的是这场吃馅饼大赛？」

===Original===
“You bet I am!”
“Is this true?” Mrs. McCall glared stonily at Archie, “Was it you who lured my poor boy into that﻿—that﻿—”
“Oh, absolutely. The fact is, don’t you know, a dear old pal of mine who runs a tobacco shop on Sixth Avenue was rather in the soup. He had backed a chappie against the champion, and the chappie was converted by one of your lectures and swore off pie at the eleventh hour. Dashed hard luck on the poor chap, don’t you know! And then I got the idea that our little friend here was the one to step in and save the situash, so I broached the matter to him. And I’ll tell you one thing,” said Archie, handsomely, “I don’t know what sort of a capacity the original chappie had, but I’ll bet he wasn’t in your son’s class. Your son has to be seen to be believed! Absolutely! You ought to be proud of him!” He turned in friendly fashion to Washy. “Rummy we should meet again like this! Never dreamed I should find you here. And, by Jove, it’s absolutely marvellous how fit you look after yesterday. I had a sort of idea you would be groaning on a bed of sickness and all that.”
There was a strange gurgling sound in the background. It resembled something getting up steam. And this, curiously enough, is precisely what it was. The thing that was getting up steam was Mr. Lindsay McCall.

===Chinese===
「可不就是！」
「这是真的吗？」麦考尔太太石像般地怒瞪着阿奇，「把我可怜的儿子诱进那个——那个——去的，是不是你？」
「哦，没错。实情是这样，您知道：我的一位亲爱的老友，在第六大道（Sixth Avenue）开着一爿烟店，那阵子相当倒霉，泡在汤里出不来。他押了一个家伙去对抗卫冕冠军，不料那家伙被您的一场演讲感化，赶在最后关头赌咒戒了馅饼。这可怜虫算倒霉透顶了，您知道！于是我灵机一动，想到咱们这位小朋友正是出面挽救局面的不二人选，便把这事向他提了一提。再告诉您一件事，」阿奇大大方方地说，「原先那位家伙肚量如何，我不得而知，可我敢打赌，他跟令郎不在一个级别上。令郎这本事，非得亲眼得见才能相信！没错！您该为他感到骄傲才是！」他友善地转向沃希。「咱们居然这样重逢，真逗！做梦也没想到会在这儿碰上你。而且，天哪，你昨天那场下来，气色居然这么棒，简直神了。我原先还隐约以为，你这会儿正卧在病榻上哼哼唧唧什么的呢。」
后台传来一阵古怪的咕噜声。那动静像是什么东西在升汽发力。而说来也怪，这恰恰正是实情。那个在升汽发力的东西，便是林赛·麦考尔先生。

===Original===
The first effect of the Washy revelations on Mr. McCall had been merely to stun him. It was not until the arrival of Archie that he had had leisure to think; but since Archie’s entrance he had been thinking rapidly and deeply.
For many years Mr. McCall had been in a state of suppressed revolution. He had smouldered, but had not dared to blaze. But this startling upheaval of his fellow-sufferer, Washy, had acted upon him like a high explosive. There was a strange gleam in his eye, a gleam of determination. He was breathing hard.
“Washy!”
His voice had lost its deprecating mildness. It rang strong and clear.

===Chinese===
沃希这一番惊爆的内幕，对麦考尔先生的第一重效果只是把他砸懵了。直到阿奇到来，他才得了思索的余裕；而自打阿奇进门，他一直在飞速而深沉地思索。
多少年来，麦考尔先生一直处在一场受压制的革命状态。他闷烧着，却不敢窜起火苗。可他的难友沃希这场惊人的变起，对他起了烈性炸药一般的效用。他眼里有一道异样的光，一道决心的光。他喘着粗气。
「沃希！」
他的嗓音失掉了那副告饶似的温和。它响得洪亮而清越。

===Original===
“Yes, pop?”
“How many pies did you eat yesterday?”
Washy considered.
“A good few.”
“How many? Twenty?”

===Chinese===
「哎，爸？」
「你昨天吃掉了多少张馅饼？」
沃希思量了一下。
「不少呢。」
「多少？二十张？」

===Original===
“More than that. I lost count. A good few.”
“And you feel as well as ever?”
“I feel fine.”
Mr. McCall dropped his glasses. He glowered for a moment at the breakfast table. His eye took in the Health Bread, the imitation coffeepot, the cereal, the nut-butter. Then with a swift movement he seized the cloth, jerked it forcibly, and brought the entire contents rattling and crashing to the floor.

===Chinese===
「比那还多。数到后来就数丢了。不少呢。」
「那你感觉跟往常一样好？」
「我好得很。」
麦考尔先生摘下了眼镜。他冲着早餐桌阴沉地瞪了片刻。他的目光把保健面包、仿咖啡壶、谷粒麦片、坚果黄油一一收在眼底。随后他手起如电，一把攥住桌布，猛力一扯，将满桌物件叮铃哐啷整个拖到了地板上。

===Original===
“Lindsay!”
Mr. McCall met his wife’s eye with quiet determination. It was plain that something had happened in the hinterland of Mr. McCall’s soul.
“Cora,” he said, resolutely, “I have come to a decision. I’ve been letting you run things your own way a little too long in this family. I’m going to assert myself. For one thing, I’ve had all I want of this food-reform foolery. Look at Washy! Yesterday that boy seems to have consumed anything from a couple of hundredweight to a ton of pie, and he has thriven on it! Thriven! I don’t want to hurt your feelings, Cora, but Washington and I have drunk our last cup of anti-caffeine! If you care to go on with the stuff, that’s your lookout. But Washy and I are through.”
He silenced his wife with a masterful gesture and turned to Archie. “And there’s another thing. I never liked the idea of that lawsuit, but I let you talk me into it. Now I’m going to do things my way. Mr. Moffam, I’m glad you looked in this morning. I’ll do just what you want. Take me to Dan Brewster now, and let’s call the thing off, and shake hands on it.”

===Chinese===
「林赛！」
麦考尔先生带着沉静的决绝迎住妻子的目光。事情很明显：麦考尔先生灵魂的腹地深处，已经出了什么变故。
「科拉，」他斩钉截铁地说，「我已拿定主意。这个家里，我容你按你那一套独断专行，容得有点太久了。我要自己当家作主了。头一件，这种饮食改革的胡闹，我已经受了个够。看看沃希！昨天那孩子吃下去的馅饼，少说一两英担、多说足足一吨，结果他反倒养壮了！养壮了！我不愿伤你的感情，科拉，可我和华盛顿已经喝下我们最后一杯抗咖啡因了！你若乐意接着灌那玩意儿，那是你自己的事。可我和沃希就此打住。」
他用一个威严的手势止住了妻子，转向阿奇。「还有一件事。那桩官司的主意，我从来就不喜欢，可我让你把我说动了。如今我要按我自己的法子办了。莫法姆先生，您今早这一趟门串得好。您要怎么办，我就怎么办。这就带我去见丹·布鲁斯特（Dan Brewster），咱们把这事一笔勾销，握手言和。」

===Original===
“Are you mad, Lindsay?”
It was Cora Bates McCall’s last shot. Mr. McCall paid no attention to it. He was shaking hands with Archie.
“I consider you, Mr. Moffam,” he said, “the most sensible young man I have ever met!”
Archie blushed modestly.
“Awfully good of you, old bean,” he said. “I wonder if you’d mind telling my jolly old father-in-law that? It’ll be a bit of news for him!”

===Chinese===
「你疯了吗，林赛？」
这是科拉·贝茨·麦考尔的最后一炮。麦考尔先生对它置若罔闻。他正在同阿奇握手。
「我认为您，莫法姆先生，」他说，「是我平生遇见过的最明白事理的年轻人！」
阿奇谦虚地红了脸。
「您可真是太好心了，老伙计，」他说。「能不能劳驾把这话也讲给我那位快活的老岳父听？在他听来，这可算得上一大新闻呢！」

## IV Work Wanted / 四 找工作

===Original===
It seemed to Archie, as he surveyed his position at the end of the first month of his married life, that all was for the best in the best of all possible worlds. In their attitude towards America, visiting Englishmen almost invariably incline to extremes, either detesting all that therein is or else becoming enthusiasts on the subject of the country, its climate, and its institutions. Archie belonged to the second class. He liked America and got on splendidly with Americans from the start. He was a friendly soul, a mixer; and in New York, that city of mixers, he found himself at home. The atmosphere of good-fellowship and the openhearted hospitality of everybody he met appealed to him. There were moments when it seemed to him as though New York had simply been waiting for him to arrive before giving the word to let the revels commence.

Nothing, of course, in this world is perfect; and, rosy as were the glasses through which Archie looked on his new surroundings, he had to admit that there was one flaw, one fly in the ointment, one individual caterpillar in the salad. Mr. Daniel Brewster, his father-in-law, remained consistently unfriendly. Indeed, his manner towards his new relative became daily more and more a manner which would have caused gossip on the plantation if Simon Legree had exhibited it in his relations with Uncle Tom. And this in spite of the fact that Archie, as early as the third morning of his stay, had gone to him and in the most frank and manly way, had withdrawn his criticism of the Hotel Cosmopolis, giving it as his considered opinion that the Hotel Cosmopolis on closer inspection appeared to be a good egg, one of the best and brightest, and a bit of all right.

“A credit to you, old thing,” said Archie cordially.

===Chinese===
婚后第一个月收官之际，阿奇盘点了一下自己的处境，觉得万事都好到了极致——在这「一切可能世界中最好的世界」里，一切都朝着最好的方向安排。来美国游览的英国人，对这个国家的态度几乎总走极端：要么把这里的一切恨得牙痒，要么摇身变成狂热信徒，对这个国家、它的气候、它的制度赞不绝口。阿奇属于后一类。他喜欢美国，而且打从一开始就和美国人打得火热。他天生就是个热络人、自来熟；而在纽约这座人人自来熟的城市里，他简直如鱼得水。遇到的每一个都一团和气、敞开怀抱慷慨相待，这种氛围正对他的胃口。有好几次他都恍惚觉得，纽约分明就是专门在等他驾到，然后一声令下，好让狂欢开场。

当然，这世上没有十全十美的事；尽管阿奇隔着一副玫瑰色的眼镜打量新环境，他也不得不承认总有一处瑕疵、香膏里的一只苍蝇、色拉里独独的一条毛毛虫。他的岳父丹尼尔·布鲁斯特（Daniel Brewster）始终不给他好脸色。说真的，他对待这位新亲戚的做派一天比一天更接近这么一种做派——当年西蒙·勒格里（Simon Legree）若是这样对待汤姆叔叔（Uncle Tom），庄园里早就传得沸沸扬扬了。而这还是碍着下面这层情面：阿奇搬进去才第三天早上，就跑到他面前，以最坦率、最有男子气概的方式，收回了自己对环球大饭店（Hotel Cosmopolis）的批评，郑重其事地申明：细看之下，环球大饭店实在是一枚好蛋，属于最上等、最光亮的那一类，相当过得去。

「这旅馆给你增光啊，老兄。」阿奇热诚地说。

===Original===
“Don’t call me old thing!” growled Mr. Brewster.

“Right-o, old companion!” said Archie amiably.

Archie, a true philosopher, bore this hostility with fortitude, but it worried Lucille.

“I do wish father understood you better,” was her wistful comment when Archie had related the conversation.

===Chinese===
「别叫我老兄！」布鲁斯特先生低吼道。

「好嘞，老同伴！」阿奇和气道。

阿奇是个名副其实的哲人，把这份敌意坚忍地扛了下来，可露西尔（Lucille）却犯了愁。

阿奇把这段对话学了一遍之后，她幽幽地念叨：「真希望爸爸能多懂你一点。」

===Original===
“Well, you know,” said Archie, “I’m open for being understood any time he cares to take a stab at it.”

“You must try and make him fond of you.”

“But how? I smile winsomely at him and whatnot, but he doesn’t respond.”

“Well, we shall have to think of something. I want him to realise what an angel you are. You *are* an angel, you know.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，你知道，」阿奇说，「他老人家哪天想来试着懂我一回，我随时奉陪。」

「你得想法子让他喜欢你。」

「可怎么个设法？我冲他妩媚地微笑，还来点儿别的小节目，可人家毫无反应。」

「那咱们只好另想辙了。我要让他明白你是多好的一个天使。你可*真是*个天使，知道吧。」

===Original===
“No, really?”

“Of course you are.”

“It’s a rummy thing,” said Archie, pursuing a train of thought which was constantly with him, “the more I see of you, the more I wonder how you can have a father like﻿—I mean to say, what I mean to say is, I wish I had known your mother; she must have been frightfully attractive.”

“What would really please him, I know,” said Lucille, “would be if you got some work to do. He loves people who work.”

===Chinese===
「不是吧，真的？」

「你当然是。」

「这事真古怪，」阿奇说，一边顺着他那挥之不去的思路想下去，「我越看你，越纳闷你怎么会有这么一位父亲——我的意思是，我想说的是，真希望我认识过你母亲；她一定迷人得要命。」

「我知道，」露西尔说，「真能让他高兴的，是你找点事做。他喜欢做事的人。」

===Original===
“Yes?” said Archie doubtfully. “Well, you know, I heard him interviewing that chappie behind the desk this morning, who works like the dickens from early morn to dewy eve, on the subject of a mistake in his figures; and, if he loved him, he dissembled it all right. Of course, I admit that so far I haven’t been one of the toilers, but the dashed difficult thing is to know how to start. I’m nosing round, but the openings for a bright young man seem so scarce.”

“Well, keep on trying. I feel sure that, if you could only find something to do, it doesn’t matter what, father would be quite different.”

It was possibly the dazzling prospect of making Mr. Brewster quite different that stimulated Archie. He was strongly of the opinion that any change in his father-in-law must inevitably be for the better. A chance meeting with James B. Wheeler, the artist, at the Pen-and-Ink Club seemed to open the way.

===Chinese===
「是吗？」阿奇将信将疑。「这个嘛，你知道，今天早上我听见他在盘问柜台后面那个伙计——那人从大清早到露水黄昏都像拼命似的干活——就为了他账目里的一处差错；就算他喜欢那伙计，也掩饰得够彻底的。当然，我承认到目前为止我还不算干活大军的一员，可难就难在不知道怎么起步。我正四处嗅探呢，只是留给聪明年轻人的空缺实在少得可怜。」

「那就接着找呀。我敢肯定，只要你能找点事做——什么事都行——爸爸就会大不一样。」

大概正是「让布鲁斯特先生大不一样」这个灿烂前景给了阿奇动力。他坚定地认为，岳父身上发生的任何变化都必然是往好里变。在笔墨俱乐部（Pen-and-Ink Club）与画家詹姆斯·B·惠勒（James B. Wheeler）的一次偶遇，似乎打开了出路。

===Original===
To a visitor to New York who has the ability to make himself liked it almost appears as though the leading industry in that city was the issuing of two-weeks’ invitation-cards to clubs. Archie since his arrival had been showered with these pleasant evidences of his popularity; and he was now an honorary member of so many clubs of various kinds that he had not time to go to them all. There were the fashionable clubs along Fifth Avenue to which his friend Reggie van Tuyl, son of his Florida hostess, had introduced him. There were the businessmen’s clubs of which he was made free by more solid citizens. And, best of all, there were the Lambs’, the Players’, the Friars’, the Coffeehouse, the Pen-and-Ink﻿—and the other resorts of the artist, the author, the actor, and the Bohemian. It was in these that Archie spent most of his time, and it was here that he made the acquaintance of J. B. Wheeler, the popular illustrator.

To Mr. Wheeler, over a friendly lunch, Archie had been confiding some of his ambitions to qualify as the hero of one of the Get-on-or-get-out-young-man-step-lively-books.

“You want a job?” said Mr. Wheeler.

===Chinese===
对一个有本事讨人喜欢的纽约访客来说，这座城市的主要产业几乎就像是签发各种俱乐部两个星期的请柬。阿奇自打登陆之日起，这类讨喜的人气凭证便如雨点般落在他身上；他如今身兼如此众多形形色色俱乐部的荣誉会员，以至于根本来不及一家家跑遍。有第五大道（Fifth Avenue）沿线那些时髦俱乐部，是他在佛罗里达下榻的那位女主人家的公子雷吉·范·图尔（Reggie van Tuyl）引荐的。有各界更殷实的公民免费放他出入的各商人俱乐部。而最妙的是，还有羔羊俱乐部（Lambs’）、演员俱乐部（Players’）、修士俱乐部（Friars’）、咖啡屋俱乐部（Coffeehouse）、笔墨俱乐部（Pen-and-Ink）——以及艺术家、作家、演员和波希米亚人的其他据点。阿奇的大部分时光就泡在这些地方，也正是在这里，他结识了那位广受欢迎的插画画家 J. B. 惠勒（J. B. Wheeler）。

某日一顿气氛友好的午餐上，阿奇向惠勒先生吐露了自己的若干抱负：他想当上某本「年轻人，要么上进要么滚蛋——动作麻利点！」式励志小说的主角。

「你想找份差事？」惠勒先生说。

===Original===
“I want a job,” said Archie.

Mr. Wheeler consumed eight fried potatoes in quick succession. He was an able trencherman.

“I always looked on you as one of our leading lilies of the field,” he said. “Why this anxiety to toil and spin?”

===Chinese===
「我想找份差事。」阿奇说。

惠勒先生一口气干掉八块煎土豆。他是位实力雄厚的大胃王。

「我一向把你算作我们数一数二的『野地里的百合花』，」他说，「怎么忽然急着要劳苦纺线啦？」

===Original===
“Well, my wife, you know, seems to think it might put me one-up with the jolly old dad if I did something.”

“And you’re not particular what you do, so long as it has the outer aspect of work?”

“Anything in the world, laddie, anything in the world.”

“Then come and pose for a picture I’m doing,” said J. B. Wheeler. “It’s for a magazine cover. You’re just the model I want, and I’ll pay you at the usual rates. Is it a go?”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，你知道，我太太似乎觉得，我要是做成了点什么，就能在那位可爱的老爸跟前占个上风。」

「而且只要外面看起来像在干活，干什么你都不挑？」

「天底下什么事都行，老兄，天底下什么事都行。」

「那就来给我正在画的一幅画当模特吧，」J. B. 惠勒说，「是给一本杂志画的封面。你正是我要的模特，我按行情付你钱。干不干？」

===Original===
“Pose?”

“You’ve only got to stand still and look like a chunk of wood. You can do that, surely?”

“I can do that,” said Archie.

“Then come along down to my studio tomorrow.”

“Right-o!” said Archie.

===Chinese===
「当模特？」

「你只需站着一动不动，摆出一截木头似的神情。这个你总行吧？」

「这个我行。」阿奇说。

「那就明天来我的画室吧。」

「好嘞！」阿奇说。
